Requested: 2026-08-08 - "fix the failing GitHub tests."

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT THE STATE IS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Two workflows:

    .github/workflows/tests.yml          Python 3.9-3.14 x six marker suites
    .github/workflows/compat-matrix.yml  wheel-only, --noconftest

Nobody has looked at an actual RUN. Everything known is inferred from local
runs, and the inference has been wrong before -- see instruction 04, where
the local suite was measuring a font scale CI never sees.

Known locally, and expected to appear in CI:

  * Five order-dependent qt failures. Each passes on its own file and fails
    in the suite. Two leaks of the same family are closed; a third -- the
    app stylesheet, left by test_photo_themes -- is confirmed and open. See
    instruction 03, which also records the two fixes that did NOT work and
    why, so they are not tried again.
  * 3.9 and 3.14 have never been run here. Every new extra was checked
    against the PyPI JSON API and resolves at both ends, and an AST scan for
    3.10+ syntax in runtime-evaluated positions found nothing. That is
    reasoning, not evidence.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY IT MATTERS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A red CI is a CI nobody reads, and a CI nobody reads is one that stops
catching the thing it was built for. It also blocks the release path: a
version bump pushed onto known-red runs produces a first result you cannot
interpret, because the pre-existing failures cannot be told from whatever
the bump broke.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT TO DO
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. LOOK AT A REAL RUN FIRST. `gh run list --limit 10` and `gh run view
   <id> --log-failed`. Do not start from the local failures; the point of
   this task is that CI and local disagree.
2. Triage into three piles and say which is which: fails locally too
   (fix the code or the test), fails only in CI (an environment
   difference -- headless, no CUDA, no NAS, a different package
   resolution), and flaky.
3. Close instruction 03 first if the five order-dependent failures show
   up. They will make everything else unreadable.
4. Fix, one pile at a time, smallest blast radius first.
5. Whatever the cause, leave the failure LOUD if it recurs. A cell that
   times out with no output -- which is what the About-dialog modal did --
   teaches nobody what went wrong.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
HOW TO KNOW IT WORKED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

`gh run list --limit 5` shows green for both workflows on `nightly`, on a
run triggered after the fix rather than a re-run of an older commit.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELIBERATELY NOT DONE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Nothing yet. Note that instruction 05 (the version bump) is deliberately
sequenced AFTER this one for the reason in WHY IT MATTERS.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-08 - THREE REAL FAULTS FOUND BY READING ACTUAL RUNS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Step 1 done: `gh run view <id> --log-failed`, not local inference. All three
faults below were invisible locally, and two of them had been failing on
every CI run for as long as the code has existed.

FIXED

  1. `_SUBMODULES` was missing eight modules that exist as files --
     classify, classify_classes, crop_source, filters, merge_tables,
     model_check, surrogate, training_basis. Added by the Classify overhaul
     and the Gate Editor work; nothing added them to the lazy loader.
     User-visible as a split that reads as flakiness: `from spacr import
     filters` works, `import spacr; spacr.filters` raises AttributeError.
     This one DOES reproduce locally -- it was simply never run.

  2. `QSettings.setPath(NativeFormat, ...)` is IGNORED on macOS and
     Windows. On macOS NativeFormat is CFPreferences, on Windows the
     registry; neither is a directory. Fixed by making the two-argument
     constructor produce INI via `setDefaultFormat`.

  3. The sandbox ROOT was not resolved while the path being checked was.
     On macOS `/var` is a symlink to `/private/var`; on Windows the 8.3
     short name resolves to the long one. Both platforms reported
     "QSettings escaped the test sandbox" for settings sitting inside it --
     the guard accusing itself.

RESULT: the compat matrix went from every platform job failing to all six
Linux jobs passing (x86-64 3.12/3.13/3.14, ARM64 3.11, plus the two
wheels-only cells and packaging metadata).

STILL FAILING: macOS arm64 / py3.11 and Windows x86-64 / py3.11, both on
the step "Packaging + smoke subset (decides this cell)". Not yet diagnosed
-- the run was still in progress when this was written, and
`--log-failed` refuses until it completes. Re-read it with:

    gh run view <id> --log-failed | grep -iE "error|assert|failed"

NOT YET LOOKED AT AT ALL: the `tests` workflow (six marker suites x 3.9-3.14)
and `docs`. Only `compat-matrix` has been read. The five order-dependent qt
failures in instruction 03 are expected to appear in `tests` and will make

THE FAILING CI JOB IS "Minimum dependencies (ubuntu-24.04, py3.9)", which
deliberately installs the lowest declared version of everything. It
resolves cellpose 4.0.1; this machine has 4.0.7, and their signatures
differ -- `use_bfloat16` exists in one and not the other, `resample`
defaults to True in one and None in the other.

SO THE SHAPE OF THIS TASK CHANGES. It is not ~85 bugs. A large share of
them are ONE unresolved question -- what is the real minimum cellpose
version -- and that question belongs to instruction 54, which already has
it recorded as "the cellpose floor is SETTLED" work.

WHAT TO DO WITH THAT: settle the floor first, re-run the matrix, and see
what is actually left. Fixing tests one at a time against a version the
project may not intend to support is work that could evaporate.

The four real product bugs found by this instruction earlier -- the
classify_merged CLI gap, the silent training-basis switch, the discarded
preview model, the mpl_toolkits phantom dependency -- were all found in
jobs OTHER than min-deps, which is the useful distinction: min-deps
failures are a version question, everything else is a code question.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-09 - THE CAUSE OF THE MIN-DEPS FAILURES IS FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The ~23 failures traced to cellpose 4.0.1 were not a version QUESTION after
all -- setup.py has required >=4.0.7 all along, and
`.github/constraints/minimum-py39.txt` pinned 4.0.1 in contradiction of it.
Raised, with a test asserting no constraint may sit below its setup.py
floor.

So the next matrix run should drop roughly 23 failures without a single
test being touched. Count what is left AFTER that run rather than from the
old log -- the earlier tally of ~85 includes them.

`test_no_tooltip_is_an_archive` enforces. Fixing one tooltip test by

AND A PROCESS NOTE THAT COST THIS TASK A DAY: every push CANCELS the
in-flight matrix. To get a complete count, push once and then STOP pushing
until the run finishes -- roughly 40 minutes, longer when GitHub's runners
are backed up. Committing locally and holding the push is the way to keep
working meanwhile.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-10 - FIVE FIXED. AND ONE OF THEM WAS MY OWN REGRESSION.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    class_balance tooltip                  named a value the code REJECTS
    two batch_correction tooltips          lost their API reference
    cell/nucleus/pathogen/organelle
      _model_name tooltips                 did not say which cellpose 4
                                           parameters still act
    save_and_add_image_to_grid docstring   lost the deliberate-reversal note

THE LAST ONE WAS SELF-INFLICTED and is worth recording. The docstring sweep
of instruction 63 CORRECTED that note -- the old text claimed
``png_dims[0]`` is the file's red channel, and the code says otherwise:

    resolve_png_channel_mapping({"png_dims": [0,1,2]}) -> {'r': 2, 'g': 1, 'b': 0}
    build_png_channels(...)  ->  source plane 0 lands in the file's BLUE slot

So the correction was right. But the old wording also carried a FACT the
correction dropped: that this mapping is the reverse of what ``png_dims``
reads like and is left that way ON PURPOSE, because every crop on disk was
written with it and flipping it would invalidate the models trained on those
crops. `test_png_channel_order_reversal_is_documented` exists to hold exactly
that, and it went red.

THE LESSON: a docstring can be WRONG and LOad-BEARING at the same time. When
correcting one, check what the old text was protecting before deleting it --
the test that guards it is the cheapest place to find out, and running the
suite for the file you edited would have caught this before it reached CI.

WHAT DOES NOT REPRODUCE LOCALLY, and is therefore version-dependent rather
than a code fault: test_cellpose_api_contract (5),
test_cellpose_channel_axis_contract (3), test_cov_io_tail,
test_cov_plot_compare_viz, test_feature_dict, test_hits. All pass here on the
installed cellpose 4.x. They need a run in the CI environment to see, which
means the next matrix run is the measurement -- not more local work.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-12 - THE "NOT REPRODUCIBLE LOCALLY" ONES WERE REPRODUCIBLE ALL ALONG
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The note above said these needed a CI run to see, "not more local work". That
was wrong, and the reason is worth keeping: THIS MACHINE HAS THE CI CELLS
ALREADY. `conda env list` shows `spacr12`, `spacr13` and `spacr14`, and
`spacr12` carries exactly the "Fast / Newest compatible" stack --

    Python 3.12.13   numpy 2.4.6   scipy 1.18.0   scikit-learn 1.9.0
    scikit-image 0.26.0   pandas 2.3.3   tifffile 2026.7.14   cellpose 4.2.1.1

-- while the default `spacr` env is Python 3.10 with scikit-learn 1.7.2. Every
failure that "did not reproduce" reproduced there in ten seconds. A second
interpreter was the missing instrument, not a 40-minute matrix run.

FIVE OF THE SIX FIXED, and only one was a stale test:

  * test_seg_qc_banner -- A REAL PRODUCT BUG ON PYTHON >= 3.12. `read_scorecard`
    detected a corrupted scorecard by letting CPython's csv module raise
    `_csv.Error: line contains NUL`. 3.12 STOPPED RAISING and parses the NUL
    into a FIELD NAME instead. The module's own NUL check looked only at
    `row.values()`, so a NUL in the header became a key it could not see:
    every real column went missing, every field took its default, `severity`
    fell back to "ok", and a damaged card came back as a clean verdict -- the
    exact invented verdict the function's docstring promises to refuse. Now
    checks the HEADER for NUL and requires `field` and `n_objects` to be
    present, so the detection no longer depends on an interpreter raising.

  * test_outlier_model (3) -- pinned numbers from a randomised estimator.
    scikit-learn 1.9 changed MinCovDet's subsampling, so the same planted
    outliers score 76-214 instead of 84-214 and the same clean 2,000 points
    flag 2 instead of 4. THE DETECTOR IS FINE: every planted point is still
    flagged, still five times the threshold past it, and 2/2000 is 0.001 --
    exactly the nominal false-positive rate this test claims to check, so the
    newer estimator is CLOSER to the contract and the equality failed on the
    improvement. Now asserts the margins and bounds instead of the digits.

  * test_settings_full_coverage -- the test's own PREMISE expired. It asserted
    `inspect.signature(print)` raises, and CPython 3.12 gave `print` a
    signature, so the uninspectable-callable fallback was never reached. It
    now DISCOVERS an uninspectable builtin at runtime and skips if the
    interpreter has none, which is the only version of this test that cannot
    break the same way again.

  * test_test_suite_hygiene -- the cellpose 4.2 padding change, same stale
    assumption already corrected in test_cellpose_channel_axis_contract.

STILL NOT REPRODUCED: test_cov_io_tail::test_convert_numpy_to_tiff_honours_limit
passes on 3.10, 3.12 AND 3.14 with the newest stack. Not a version difference,
so the remaining hypothesis is test ORDER -- CI runs the whole suite with
pytest-randomly and these runs use `-p no:randomly`. `pytest-randomly` is not
installed in the 3.12 env, so confirming it needs either that plugin there or
the CI run itself.

THE GENERAL LESSON, which applies past this instruction: "does not reproduce
locally" is a statement about the environment that was used, not about the
failure. Before concluding a failure needs CI, check whether the machine can
be made to look like CI -- here it already did.

2026-08-25 - RE-TRIAGED FROM A FRESH RUN, AND THE QT HANG IS DIAGNOSED

Read off runs 32877097426 / 32877097614 / 32877097390 -- a push, not a
re-run -- and every failure below was reproduced locally before anything
was changed.

WHAT WENT GREEN ON ITS OWN, and why the old triage above is worth nothing

  Six of the seven non-Qt jobs were failing on ONE thing: two test modules
  imported names their product code did not have yet
  (`spacr.qt.screens.mask`, `setup_slides.ANIMATION_LABEL`). Tests were
  committed a commit ahead of the code they test, so a collection error
  took down Fast, Slow, Network, NAS, GPU, Integration and min-deps at
  once. Both landed in the next two commits and all six cleared.

  compat-matrix's eight-cell failure went the same way: `_SUBMODULES` is
  now DERIVED FROM THE DIRECTORY rather than hand-listed, which is the
  ending this file asked for after the fourth recurrence, and
  `EXPECTED_LAZY` dropped the eleven retired Tk modules. Every Linux and
  Windows cell is green.

  THE LESSON, and it is the same one this file keeps re-learning from the
  other side: a triage is only worth the commit it was taken on. Most of
  what the last section listed as work was already fixed by the time it
  was read.

THE QT SHARD DOES NOT HANG ANY MORE, and the diagnosis was right

  Both shards now run to a summary. Shard 0 fell from 65 minutes to 34.
  What the suite reported at teardown -- a QApplication holding tens of
  thousands of widgets -- was the cause, and it is now measured rather
  than inferred.

  THE FIRST INSTRUMENT WAS WRONG, which is worth recording because it
  pointed at 200 innocent files. Counting live widgets at session end
  measures mostly widgets that are already DEAD: `deleteLater` posts an
  event, and a headless file may never spin the loop that delivers it.
  Measured that way 205 files "leaked" 9,300 windows between them. Deliver
  the deletions and collect first, and 199 of those 205 leak NOTHING. The
  number to measure is the residue after a flush, never the raw count.

  WHAT ACTUALLY RETAINS A TREE: a top-level widget that is never CLOSED
  cannot be freed at all. Built, dropped and collected any number of
  times, one `AppScreen` leaves 298 windows and 1,256 widgets standing;
  closed first, one collect-then-deliver round takes it to nought. The
  cycle runs through connections Qt owns on the C++ side, where Python's
  collector cannot follow, so `closeEvent` -- which stops the heartbeat
  timer -- is the only thing that breaks it.

  THE BIG ONE WAS NOT A TEST FILE AT ALL. `tests/qt/conftest.py` delivers
  pending deletions and bounds the retained tree at every test boundary,
  and it works -- but a conftest reaches its own directory, and the `qt`
  marker is far wider than `tests/qt`. Over a hundred modules directly
  under `tests/` carry `@pytest.mark.qt`, build real widgets, and ran with
  none of that housekeeping. Thirty of them in one process, 330 tests:

                    peak windows   peak widgets   left at the end   wall
      before             5,945         39,713          3,161        98.5s
      after                797          5,709            158        95.0s

  The same fixture now lives in `tests/conftest.py`, ordered behind the
  QSettings sandbox because destroying a widget can write a preference,
  and free for non-Qt tests -- it reads one entry in `sys.modules` and
  never imports PySide6. It is not slower; it is slightly faster, because
  the tree it prevents is the tree every later restyle had to walk.

  AND TWO FIXTURES THAT NEVER DISPOSED OF WHAT THEY BUILT, found by the
  corrected instrument and fixed by handing the widget to `qtbot`:

      test_colour_by_takes_more_than_one_column   1,760 windows -> 0
      test_cov_4_grouped_plot                        75 windows -> 0
      test_every_figure_is_a_pyqtgraph_that_retypes  295 windows -> 75

  Across 466 of the 478 files in `tests/qt`, run one per process, the
  residue that survives a full flush totalled 6,788 windows and 60,679
  widgets before the three fixes above. What is left is concentrated
  rather than spread:

      1,020  test_the_volcano_takes_more_than_one_guide
        812  test_the_bottom_panels_collapse
        326  test_cov_qt_app
        302  test_home_layout
        298  test_force_restart_comes_back_where_you_were
        298  test_settings_column_never_black
        298  test_settings_column_panel
        298  test_preview_registry

        147  test_every_live_tile_opens

  Note the signature that repeats exactly: 298 windows made of 220 QMenu,
  48 QFrame and 22 pyqtgraph ViewBoxMenu, which is ONE `AppScreen`. Every
  file showing it already registers what it builds with `qtbot`, so the
  screen that survives is one the product builds and holds -- most likely
  through `MainWindow` -- not one a test forgot. That is the next thread
  to pull and it is product-side, not a test fix.

2026-08-25 - THE CI STACK IS NOT THE DEVELOPER STACK, AND ONE TEST SAID SO
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Fast and Minimum dependencies were down to ONE failure each, and it was the
same test both times:

    tests/test_an_object_is_measured_in_its_own_box.py
        ::test_an_intensity_percentile_keeps_the_dtype_of_its_image

FIXED, and the cause is worth more than the fix.

`measure._percentiles_of` batches the six percentile cut points into one
`np.percentile` call for integers and float64, and deliberately does NOT
batch a narrow float, because numpy computes the sequence form of a float32
input in float64 and the scalar form in float32; batching would move the
value a measured database already holds. The test asserted the resulting
column is `float32`.

That premise is TRUE ON NUMPY 2.4 AND LATER AND FALSE ON NUMPY 1.26, which
computes BOTH forms in float64. The product is right on both -- it always
takes the scalar form for a narrow float, so the number never moves -- but
the dtype is numpy's to choose, not spaCR's. The test now reads the
reference dtype off numpy instead of writing `float32`, and a second test
counts the calls, which is the batching decision itself and is the same on
every numpy.

WHY CI HAD numpy 1.26 AT ALL, and this is the part to keep:

    Every job in tests.yml installs the `attribution` extra. Every torchcam
    release declares `numpy<2.0.0`. So pip resolves numpy 1.26.4 in ALL of
    them -- Fast, Slow, Qt, Network, NAS, GPU, Integration and min-deps --
    while this machine develops on numpy 2.5.2.

    NO JOB IN tests.yml EVER RUNS numpy 2. compat-matrix does (it asks for
    `qt,dev,zernike` and never `attribution`), but that workflow is a
    smoke subset with --noconftest, not the suite.

    That is the whole shape of "passes locally, fails in CI" for anything
    numerical, and it is the third time this file has recorded a version
    difference as if it were a mystery. The instrument that settles one in
    ten seconds is `np.__version__` in both places.

    Closing the gap costs one job: the Fast suite again with
    `extras: "qt,dev,zernike,btrack,czi"` -- no attribution, therefore
    numpy 2.x. NOT added here, because adding an unmeasured job to a
    workflow being driven to green is a decision for the maintainer.

THE QT HANG IS NOT FIXED. IT MOVED, AND IT WAS INVISIBLE BY DESIGN.

Shard 1 did not run to a summary. It reached 99% in about 40 minutes, then
printed NOTHING for 74.8 minutes until the 120-minute job timeout killed
it. The runner then reported five orphan python processes at cleanup.

The last test to start and never report:

    tests/test_the_sweep_says_whether_the_cap_is_real.py
        ::test_the_sweep_panel_puts_it_on_screen

It passes alone in five seconds.

WHY NOTHING WAS PRINTED, which is the reusable workflow's own bug: the
suite runs with `-o faulthandler_timeout=900`, and the comment above that
step claimed a hang would therefore name itself. It cannot under `-n`. The
dump is written by the WORKER, and xdist forwards a worker's output only
when the worker reports a result, so a worker that never reports never
delivers its dump. The one mechanism meant to make a hang loud is the one a
hang disables.

FIXED IN .github: a `per_test_timeout_seconds` input, 1200 on the Qt
shards. pytest-timeout's thread method does not need the wedged test to
come back to the interpreter -- a watchdog dumps every stack and ends the
worker, and xdist reports the crash against the test id. Measured on a
deliberately wedged test: the id is named, the surviving workers finish
their own files, and the session prints a summary.

    `--max-worker-restart=0` goes with it and is not optional. Without it
    xdist replaces the ended worker and hands it the SAME test, which wedges
    again: measured, a 3-second ceiling was paid NINE times. A deterministic
    hang would then cost more than the silence it replaced.

WHAT THE HANG PROBABLY IS, not yet proved and product-side:
`spacr.parameter_sweep.containment_available` (spacr/parameter_sweep.py:673)
runs `systemd-run --user --scope ... true` through
`subprocess.run(capture_output=True, timeout=10)`. That timeout kills the
direct child only and then blocks in `communicate()` until every inherited
pipe is closed, with no second bound. A scope that outlives the killed
`systemd-run` holds those pipes open, which matches both the silence and
the orphan processes. It is the only unguarded external call the test
makes. Fix shape: `Popen` with `kill()` then `wait(timeout=...)`, or read
the pipes with a bounded second wait, so the ten seconds actually bound the
call.

A NEW HYGIENE VIOLATION IS ALREADY QUEUED TO TURN Fast RED AGAIN. Two
CellposeModel doubles landed with `def eval(self, x, **kwargs)`:

    tests/qt/test_the_make_masks_cellpose_detect.py:237  class Variadic
    tests/qt/test_the_make_masks_cellpose_detect.py:370  class Boom

The ratchet is empty with a ceiling of 0, so these are failures rather than
entries, and the ratchet is NOT the thing to move: a double that accepts any
axis cannot tell a working call from the one that crashed every real run.
The rule states the fix -- `def eval(self, x,
channel_axis=MISSING_CHANNEL_AXIS, **kwargs)`, handed to
`check_cellpose_eval_call` in tests/conftest.py.

DOCS IS RED FOR A REASON THAT CANNOT BE FIXED MID-RUN

Layer 1 is unchanged and still exactly five records; a scan of all 1,391
reviewed API records finds those five and nothing else. Layer 2 has grown
from 66 stale symbols to 325, plus 270 public symbols the English manifest
has never seen and 12 it still lists that no longer exist.

That growth is the finding. A reviewed record pins the exact English text
of a docstring, and this run is rewriting docstrings and tooltips
wholesale, so the audit goes stale again with every commit -- which is why
docs has been red on every push for days. It is settled LAST, from a
finished tree, not now.

4. TWO PRODUCT DEFECTS FOUND WHILE REPRODUCING, both in
   spacr/qt/settings_search.py and neither of them a test problem.

   THE SEARCH STRIP MAKES ITS SCREEN CRASH WHEN IT IS DESTROYED. Minimal,
   deterministic, and nothing to do with Cellpose:

       app = QApplication([])
       screen = AppScreen("mask")          # any key: measure, classify...
       spacr.qt.settings_search.install(screen)
       screen.deleteLater()
       QCoreApplication.sendPostedEvents(None, QEvent.DeferredDelete)
       # SIGSEGV

   Without the `install` line the same screen is destroyed cleanly. Under
   gdb the crash is `Shiboken::BindingManager::releaseWrapper` inside
   `QObjectPrivate::deleteChildren()` -- a wrapper released twice, which
   points at `install` reparenting the screen's own `_settings_scroll` out
   of the splitter and into a container while the screen keeps holding it.
   This is what takes tests/qt/test_cellpose_workbench.py down: the test
   BODY passes and the process dies afterwards, which xdist reports as a
   failed test with no message.

   "ALL SETTINGS" NO LONGER RESTORES EVERY ROW. On the mask screen the
   strip indexes 350 keys and shows 333 at the ALL level. The seventeen it
   cannot restore are the whole z-stack and t-stack groups -- z_stack,
   z_segmentation_mode, z_axis, z_projection, anisotropy, voxel_size_z_um,
   voxel_size_xy_um, stitch_threshold, t_stack, t_axis_order, t_axis,
   frame_interval_s, t_track_backend, t_link_threshold,
   t_max_displacement_px, t_max_displacement_um, t_project_for_tracking.
   `z_stack` and `t_stack` are the master toggles that would turn their
   own groups on, so an applicability rule is hiding the control that
   satisfies it. Two tests in tests/qt/test_settings_search.py say so.

5. FAST AND MINIMUM DEPENDENCIES RUN 19 BATCHES OF 54, AND THAT IS THE
   BIGGEST THING BETWEEN THIS FILE AND GREEN.

   Both jobs run `tools/run_pytest_batches.py`, which splits the file list
   into fresh pytest processes to keep resident memory under the runner's
   limit. It returns as soon as one batch fails:

       if result.returncode not in (0, NO_TESTS_COLLECTED):
           return int(result.returncode)

   The log says so plainly once you look for it -- `pytest batch 19/54` is
   the last line either job printed. The failing test,
   tests/test_api_i18n_extractor.py, is IN batch 19. Thirty-five batches,
   about two thirds of the fast partition, were never executed.

   So "Fast has one failure" is not a measurement, it is a floor. Proof
   rather than inference: tests/test_instruction_index.py sits in batch 39
   and had THREE failures on this very commit -- instruction files 245, 246
   and 247 were filed and 244 finished without `00_INDEX.txt` being
   regenerated, and the index has been stale in the tree since bdc60f66
   with CI silent about it the whole time. Regenerating the index is part
   of this change; the reason CI never said so is not.

   This is also why every push produces a DIFFERENT red list: fixing the
   test that stops batch N reveals batch N+1, one commit at a time, and a
   triage taken from such a run describes a prefix of the suite rather than
   the suite.

   THE FIX, which is two lines in a file this instruction does not own:
   give `run_pytest_batches.py` a `--keep-going` flag -- default OFF so no
   existing caller changes -- that records the worst exit status instead of
   returning on it, and pass `--keep-going` from the `fast` and
   `minimum-dependencies` steps in .github/workflows/tests.yml. A job that
   stops at the first failure cannot answer the question this instruction
   exists to answer.

  docs            red on a THIRD layer in front of the two: six reviewed
                  runtime records for settings the registry has dropped.

HOW TO MEASURE THE RETAINED TREE, so the next reader does not rebuild it

  Run one file per process with a plugin that, at `pytest_sessionfinish`,
  delivers `QEvent.DeferredDelete` and collects three times BEFORE
  counting `QApplication.topLevelWidgets()`. The count before that flush
  is not a leak and will send you after the wrong files.

2026-09-15 (later) - THE REST OF THE a5ef1cd7b RUN: ONE TIMING TEST, THREE MORE CRASHES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The run completed at 04:12 as failure. Beyond the jobs above, Fast and
Minimum dependencies failed. Read from their logs:

  Fast AND Min-deps   test_the_outline_is_not_recomputed_for_nothing ::
        test_changing_a_display_setting_reuses_the_outline -- "4.15 ms per
        crop" and "4.94 ms per crop" against an absolute `moved < 2.0`. The
        redraw recomputed nothing; the runner is slower than the workstation
        the number was written on (0.31 ms here). FIXED: the redraw is now
        timed against the same redraw with the outline cache emptied before
        every crop (3.0 ms here, about ten times), threshold a third of it.
        A first attempt against a single cold pass was VACUOUS -- the first
        draw in a process costs ~140 ms of one-time work -- and a negative
        control (a draw_crop that always recomputes) caught it; the shipped
        version fails that control (3.02 against 2.99 ms).

  Min-deps   test_known_bug_contracts :: test_cluster_grid_uses_the_panel_...
             test_the_last_three_charts_are_pyqtgraph :: test_the_scene_...
             test_cov_w2_4_metadata_mapper :: test_a_cancelled_dialog_stops_...
        All three are the crash class again: each test PASSED and then
        "worker 'gwN' crashed while running" it (the metadata mapper one with
        "Fatal Python error: Segmentation fault"). Five crash sites tonight,
        none sharing a Python-level cause. The work session measured and ruled
        out spacr/qt/path_probe.py in three shapes (its four threads are the
        designed WORKERS=4 pool, signals are queued to the GUI thread, and
        destroyed receivers are guarded).

  Fast also printed three "Timeout (0:05:00)!" dumps from the conftest
  watchdog (tests/conftest.py line 89) without failing a test on them.

  A FAMILY, NOT TWO ONE-OFFS (both sessions, 2026-09-15). Three reds tonight
  were assertions that measured the machine or compared against nothing:
  the outline redraw's absolute 2.0 ms, and the work session's two magic-wand
  tests with absolute millisecond thresholds. All three were fixed the same
  way -- time against a CONTROL in the same process (a forced recompute, the
  same call without the cache) so the runner's speed cancels out. And three
  times one of us caught a replacement that was itself vacuous by MUTATING it
  (a negative control that must fail) rather than by reading it: the first
  outline fix compared against a single cold pass that carries ~140 ms of
  one-time cost and would have passed a redraw that recomputed everything.
  Rule for this suite: no absolute wall-clock threshold in a test that runs
  on shared CI, and no timing assertion without a negative control run once.

2026-09-15 (07:50 UTC) - A SIXTH CRASH SITE, REPRODUCED LOCALLY AND SERIALLY, AND NOT ATTRIBUTED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Found by the work session: a plain serial `pytest tests/qt -q -p no:randomly`
  (offscreen, no xdist, no CI) segfaulted at about 7%, exit 139, core dumped.
  The current thread was a FIXTURE, not a test:

      tests/qt/conftest.py:179  _restore_font_scale -> app.setStyleSheet(...)

  with the four spacr/qt/path_probe.py workers (the designed WORKERS=4 pool,
  ruled out in three shapes) and the conftest watchdog alive. The comment
  above that line says the fixture exists to stop old suite crashes by
  applying a saved style only after pytest-qt's deleteLater queue drains.

  NOT ATTRIBUTED, AND RECORDED WITH THE RETRACTION ON PURPOSE. Three runs of
  the same command on the same tree and machine:

      run 1   fee40b72a's parentless-Polish skip ON    segfault at 7%
      run 2   skip OFF                                  clean to 13% (stopped
                                                        there; question answered)
      run 3   skip ON                                   clean to 23%, then the
                                                        2,400 s timeout (exit 124)

  After runs 1-2 the skip was named as the cause and a revert was announced;
  run 3, the confirmation, did not crash. At n=3 the crash is
  NONDETERMINISTIC and the evidence supports NO attribution. fee40b72a is not
  reverted. (Its separate field_fade regression is real, understood, and fixed
  with its contract written as a test: an unshown parentless widget renders
  without the sheet -- show it or parent it.)

  WHAT FOLLOWS FOR THE CRASH CLASS: the five CI sites above predate fee40b72a
  (a5ef1cd7b, 988a4fa7d) and all look alike -- a worker dying in unrelated
  code a few tests after something else. A nondeterministic crash inside a
  teardown re-polish is ONE phenomenon consistent with all six.
  HYPOTHESIS, UNPROVEN: `app.setStyleSheet()` re-polishes every live widget;
  one Python wrapper whose C++ object is already gone is enough to corrupt
  memory, and the damage surfaces later in whatever runs next.

  Run 3 went more than three times past the point where run 1 crashed. 1 crash
  in 2 skip-on runs: nondeterministic, unattributed.

  AND A FACT THAT OUTLIVES THE HYPOTHESIS: `tests/qt` CANNOT be run end to end
  in one process inside a CI job's budget -- 23% in 2,400 s puts the whole
  directory near three hours serially. That is why it is sharded, and why a
  crash that needs one process to live long enough was invisible: "all Qt
  tests pass" has meant "every shard passed separately" for the life of this
  suite.

  2026-09-15 (09:05 UTC) -- COMPAT-MATRIX SUCCESS ON b740c741d, the first green
  run of that workflow in the history read above, and the first run carrying
  32f701850's 70-minute step timeout. docs is SUCCESS on b740c741d as well, so
  docs has now passed on two consecutive nightly heads (bac9151d0, b740c741d).

  A TRAP FOR RATCHETS, FOUND WHILE CATALOGUING mark_to_start_on (2026-09-15):
  tests/test_api_i18n_extractor.py pinned the AutoAPI boundary as
  `10_696 - len(docs) == 163`. Measured with `_is_rendered_autoapi_entry`
  neutralised, the unfiltered inventory was already 10,751 on 2026-09-14 and
  the pin still passed, because rendered symbols grew the pre-filter count and
  len(docs) by the same amount while the note above it claimed "the autoapi
  total is unchanged at 10,696". It is now `10_752 - len(docs) == 218`, with
  the filtered buckets summed (116+59+16+16+5+4+1+1 = 218) and re-checked by
  the work session. A test that pins the DIFFERENCE between two numbers can be
  wrong on both sides and stay green: pin each side against a measurement.

  CRASH RATE, MEASURED BY THE WORK SESSION (2026-09-15, 10:25 UTC). Serial
  `pytest tests/qt -q -p no:randomly`, same tree and machine, counting ONLY runs
  that reached or passed the crash point (7%):

      original (skip on)        crashed at 7%
      skip off                  clean to 13%
      skip on, confirmation     clean to 23%
      rate run 1                clean to 8%
      rate run 2                clean to 8%
      rate run 3                crashed at 7%

      2 crashes in 6 qualifying runs -- roughly one in three.

  Both crashes are at 7%, in `_restore_font_scale -> app.setStyleSheet`: the
  SITE is deterministic and the OCCURRENCE is not, which is the shape of a
  teardown race, not of a bad line. At a one-in-three base rate, the original
  "crash with the skip, clean without" was a coin flip that two runs could never
  have separated.

  THE MORE USEFUL LESSON: the first attempt at a rate was six runs reporting
  "0 crashes", and it measured NOTHING -- every run hit a 600 s cap at 6%, short
  of the 7% crash point, while competing for CPU. A probe that never reaches the
  event cannot report its rate. The second attempt's own summary then mislabelled
  the crashing run as "short of 7%" because it keyed on reaching 8%, and a crash
  stops a run at 7%: a crash is the event regardless of depth. Report reached
  depth beside every crash count.

      GPU an earlier cuda:0 survives (test_cov_object_preprocess_segment,
      test_cov_object_organelle_sam x3, analysis_submodules cellpose,
      test_cov_w3_7_cli_remote 'cuda:0' == 'cpu'). CI has no GPU and never
      sees it. Being fixed with a per-test reset (wip/43-accelerator-cache).
    * THE CONFTEST MEMORY GUARD LOOKS LIKE A CRASH: a worker whose RSS passes
      SPACR_TEST_MEMORY_GB (default 6) calls os._exit(3), and pytest-xdist logs
      that as "node down: Not properly terminated" -- the same words as a
      segfault, but with no "Fatal Python error" line. Every crash site must be
      classified by whether a fatal error and faulthandler stack was printed;
      an exit without one is the guard, and points at a test needing >6 GB, not
      at the crash family.
    * the README checkout size (above).

  A LEAD ON THE CRASH FAMILY, being tested (wip/43-gc-cycles): aad90d8f0 found
  widgets holding a STRONG reference to their own window (ambient, dna_rain,
  first_run, shortcuts, drawer). Only Python's cyclic GC can free such a pair,
  and it empties the widget before Qt destroys it; with that fix reverted three
  of its tests segfaulted pytest itself. Whether GC-freed Qt cycles explain the
  unattributed worker segfaults is the hypothesis -- not a finding until a
  forced-GC run reproduces a recorded site and the fix removes it.

  THE GPU-MACHINE DEVICE LEAK, FIXED (90176bb03). The work session's report
  was right about the leak and partly wrong about its victims. The main
  polluter was tests/conftest.py itself: it cached the machine's real cuda:0
  once per session and restored it after every test, so the victims failed even
  alone on a GPU machine; test_cellpose4_model_story.py was a second polluter.
  Real victims: test_cov_object_preprocess_segment::test_load_unet_model_loads_on
  _cpu_in_eval_mode and three in test_cov_object_organelle_sam (the 'cuda:0' ==
  'cpu' message is organelle_sam's -- the w3_7 CLI attribution was wrong, and a
  grep for "cuda.is_available" misses organelle_sam). Fix: a private
  spacr.accelerator._reset_device_cache(), called by the fixture before and
  after each test only if the module is already imported. Proof: the polluter
  order fails 4 on nightly, passes after, fails again with the fixture off;
  1,552 non-Qt and 251 Qt pass with and without a GPU. CI has no GPU, so CI
  never saw any of it -- a local-only red, but it cost a work-session run.

  NOT THIS LEAK, AND STILL OPEN: the analysis_submodules cellpose test is a
  STALE STRICT XFAIL -- its reason (bsize) is gone from spacr/submodules.py.
  Candidates by grep: tests/test_cov_submodules_cellpose_train_test.py tests/test_analysis_submodules_real_data.py tests/test_cov_object_masks_sam.py . A strict xfail whose defect was
  fixed turns into an XPASS failure; retire it with the fix it recorded.

2026-09-15 (evening) - THE GC-CYCLE LEAD, TESTED: ONE DETERMINISTIC CRASH FOUND

Branch wip/43-gc-cycles on aad90d8f0 (the backdrop weakref fix). Local stack:

session scratchpad (gcforce.py); nothing of it is committed.

THE LEAD'S HYPOTHESIS: Qt object graphs freed by Python's cycle collector,
rather than by Qt parent ownership, segfault the workers, at a site that looks
unrelated because the collector runs at an arbitrary allocation.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED FOR ONE CRASH, NOT SHOWN FOR THE RECORDED CI SITES.

4. THE CRASH THAT DID REPRODUCE, AND ITS CAUSE

  tests/qt/test_cov_r5_cell_montage_view.py followed by tests/qt/test_one_close_mark.py
  segfaults at test_one_close_mark.py:780, the first qapp.setStyleSheet in
  test_the_mark_re_measures_when_zoom_changes_under_it. On clean aad90d8f0 in
  3 of 3 runs; either file alone passes. gdb: SIGSEGV in libQt6Widgets under
  PySide's setStyleSheet, no collector frames.

  automatic GC on                       crash, 12 of 12 (3 on clean aad90d8f0)
  automatic GC off for the session      pass 2/2
  gc.collect() at every test boundary   pass 1/1
  GC on only for tests 47-50            crash (the 41,135-object backlog freed
                                        at 47's setup)
  same, but DEBUG_SAVEALL (kept, not    pass: freeing it is necessary
    freed)
  every pyqtgraph ViewBox/ViewBoxMenu   pass 2/2
    kept alive
  only their menus retired at teardown  pass 2/2 (the ViewBox itself still
                                        freed by the collector)

  The collector callbacks, written to a raw file descriptor because pytest's
  capture swallows stderr on a crash, show no collection during the crashing
  call. The damage is done earlier: a lifetime tracker showed the montage's
  Compare tab (GroupedPlot, a FastPlot) building two ViewBoxMenu in test 16,
  and the collector freeing both in test 35, inside a gen1 collection triggered
  from ast.parse. Twelve tests later the app-wide restyle crashes.

  WHY THEY WERE LEFT TO THE COLLECTOR: pyqtgraph makes PlotItem.ctrlMenu and
  ViewBox.menu parentless top-level windows. FastPlot.closeEvent retires them
  through widget_cleanup, but an EMBEDDED plot never receives closeEvent -- it
  is destroyed with its parent, or detached and deleteLater'd by
  MeasurementComparePanel._draw.

  FIXED: FastPlot connects its `destroyed` to each menu's own deleteLater (a Qt
  receiver connection: no Python slot, nothing captures the plot, and Qt drops
  it when closeEvent already retired the menu). Commit cf29973ab. The pair now
  passes 5 of 5 with automatic GC on. New test
  tests/qt/test_an_embedded_plot_takes_its_menus_with_it.py: 2 of its 4 fail
  before (menus still alive 2 s after the plot is gone), all pass after.
  Regression: 17 plot/cleanup/compare/montage files 227 passed; the six
  close-mark files 102 passed; every tests/qt file naming FastPlot or its
  subclasses (37 files) 733 passed.

5. WHAT FOLLOWS

  * The lead's MECHANISM is real in this suite: a Qt graph freed by the cycle
    collector at an arbitrary allocation corrupted state that an unrelated
    restyle hit twelve tests later, with no collector on the crashing stack.
    That is the shape of every CI site.
  * It is NOT shown to be what killed the recorded CI workers. None of the six
    CI sites crashed locally under forced collection except as described
    above, and the one deterministic local crash was not one of them. Do not
    close the family on this.
  * Two open leads, measured and unfixed: (a) worker-thread collection of the
    settings-model widget graph (3/3 with an explicit worker collect, frame
    identical to the CI workbench crash); the test process has automatic GC on
    every thread, while the app switches it to the GUI thread (gc_policy), and
    the CI dropzone stacks both show a second thread allocating heavily at the
    moment of death. (b) CommandPalette's window that is never freed.
  * Harness option NOT taken: gc_policy in the test process (automatic GC off,
    GUI-thread collection at boundaries). It passed everything it was run on,
    but the serial A/B had no baseline crash to remove, so it is unproven.
2026-09-15 (evening) - A DEAD WORKER TOOK ITS COVERAGE WITH IT; QT SHARD 0'S
                       LOST RUNNER NOW LOGS MEMORY, AND LOCALLY IT NEARS 16 GB
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Branch wip/288-ci-crash-tolerant: 55b97f378, cf6acebc3 (claim 3ddd933d1).

  COVERAGE (details in item 288). A worker killed by a signal, or by the
  conftest memory guard (exit 3), never writes its coverage data; measured on a
  toy, a PASSED test's lines vanished with its worker. That is how run
  34989909231's shard-0 and shard-9 segfaults became thirteen false ratchet
  regressions. Coverage batches now re-run a lost worker's files serially; an
  unrecovered loss fails the gate as an INCOMPLETE MEASUREMENT (exit 3), named
  by exit status: SIGSEGV (this item's crash family) or exit 3 (memory guard).
  The crash still fails its shard; nothing here attributes it.

  QT TELEMETRY. Input memory_telemetry_seconds (Qt: 30) runs
  tools/ci_memory_telemetry.sh beside pytest: a MEMORY line every 30 s in the
  LIVE log (used/available, swap, PSI, oom_kill count, free disk, every pytest
  process's RSS beside guard=6GB, top 4 processes), a line for each pytest
  process that vanished with its last RSS, and fuller samples uploaded and
  printed on failure. A lost runner keeps the lines already streamed.

  LOCAL EVIDENCE for the memory hypothesis (spacr env, CUDA hidden, the
  CI-identical shard 0 command with -n 4, process-tree PSS every 15 s):

      t (s)   shard   tree PSS MiB   tree RSS MiB
        183     10%        8,711         10,343
        381     26%        9,760         11,439
        979     53%       14,297         15,979
       1502    ~68%       15,091         16,813   peak; workers 4.2/3.8/3.7/3.1 GB PSS
       1517     68%       11,410         12,632   gw1 segfaulted, 3 workers left

  GitHub's standard public-repository Linux runner has 16 GB RAM. The pytest
  tree alone reached 92% of 16 GiB by ~68%, still climbing; CI lost the runner
  at 83% and 96%. Consistent with memory exhaustion, not proof (different
  stack and CPU; PSS is not the VM's accounting); the next dispatched run's
  MEMORY lines decide it.

  NINTH CRASH SITE, LOCAL: that run's gw1 took "Fatal Python error:
  Segmentation fault" in tests/qt/test_cov_2_data_manager.py::test_accepting_
  the_archive_question_moves_and_leaves_the_record (path_probe workers alive),
  and --max-worker-restart=0 ended the shard: 8,255 passed, 5 failed in 28:39.

  PROPOSAL, NOT APPLIED. Each long-lived worker grew ~2.3 -> 4.2 GB over the
  shard, so memory scales with workers x tests per worker. Three workers cut
  the total by about a quarter but give each a third more tests (extrapolated
  ~5.6 GB, against the 6 GB guard). A third Qt shard, or fresh-worker batches
  as the coverage shards use, lowers both. Decide on one run's MEMORY lines.

2026-09-15 (evening) - THE gw1 "Fatal Python error: Aborted" IN RUN 34989909231 IS TORCH, NOT QT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Job "Minimum dependencies (ubuntu-24.04, py3.9)", worker gw1, in
  test_a_committed_row_outranks_the_translation_cache.py::
  test_a_repair_mode_retranslates_the_rows_it_targets. No Qt frame on the
  faulthandler stack; the current thread ended in
  tools/build_i18n_catalogs.py:5647 _translate_batches,
  `torch.set_num_interop_threads(1)`. THIS SITE OF THE CRASH FAMILY IS A TORCH
  INTER-OP-THREADS ABORT, not Qt, and counts for no Qt hypothesis.

  Mechanism: torch sizes its inter-op pool once per process, and the helper
  set it on every model-reaching call. The test before it in the same worker
  had already reached the (stub) model. torch 2.13 raises RuntimeError on the
  second call and an `except RuntimeError` swallowed it, so no local run could
  see it. The min-deps pin, torch 2.1.0+cpu, lets the c10::Error escape the
  binding: "terminate called after throwing an instance of 'c10::Error'",
  SIGABRT, uncatchable.

  Proof in a torch 2.1.0+cpu venv: that pair, serial in one process, aborts at
  line 5647 on 8e26e209d and passes after the fix. Fix (wip/43-interop-threads):
  _limit_torch_interop_threads asks torch at most once per process and not at
  all when it already reports 1. Test:
  tests/test_a_second_translation_call_does_not_abort_the_process.py (two calls
  after a torch op, in a subprocess): before the fix -6/SIGABRT on 2.1.0 and a
  second call counted on 2.13; after it, green on both. This was the only
  set_num_interop_threads call in spacr/ and tools/.

2026-09-20 -- COMPAT-MATRIX WENT RED AFTER ITEM 400, AND THE CITATION HAD THE
              WRONG RELEASE'S DOI
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The status at the top of this file records compat-matrix as GREEN. It has
been failing since item 400's commit b043fcbc7, and the reason is worth
writing down because nothing was actually broken.

(1) `test_the_torchcam_python_313_limit_is_written_down` read `setup.py`
looking for the string `numpy<2.0.0`, because that is where the reason was
written -- in a COMMENT. Item 400 moved setup.py's 1,156 comments to
`docs/notes/setup.md`, and the string went with them. The test's own message
says "do not silently delete the reason it remains outside `all`", and
nobody had: `docs/notes/setup.md` explains it at length in three places.
The test now reads the notes, and checks that the file names both the pin
and torchcam, so a reader who greps for either finds the reason. The
BEHAVIOURAL half is untouched and was always green --
`test_attribution_extra_is_not_in_all` holds that torchcam is in the
`attribution` extra and out of `all`.

(2) `test_the_citation_version_doi_is_the_one_for_the_version_it_claims`
found exactly what it was written to find. CITATION.cff said
`version: "1.5.0.8"` while its version DOI, `10.5281/zenodo.22726094`, is
described as "spaCR 1.5.0.7" -- `packaging/release.py` rewrites `version`
and `date-released` and leaves `doi` behind, which is the failure
`packaging/README.md` names. This test is SKIPPED on compat-matrix (that
job installs build tooling only and has no PyYAML), so CI could not have
caught it; it failed here.

THE OUTSIDE WORLD WAS ASKED RATHER THAN GUESSED, which is section 1's rule.
Zenodo's API, queried on the concept DOI `10.5281/zenodo.21343316`, lists
ten versions and gives v1.5.0.8 as `10.5281/zenodo.22797833`, published
2026-09-16 -- the same date CITATION.cff already carried. Both the
top-level `doi:` and the version identifier now name it.

THE 3.15 CELL IS NOT A FAILURE OF THIS ITEM. "3.15 installs, from wheels
only" is red because scikit-image publishes no cp315 wheels yet, and the
job already carries `continue-on-error: ${{ matrix.python-version ==
'3.15' }}` with the reason beside it: the cell is informational until the
ecosystem admits that interpreter. Nothing in .github was touched.

MEASURED: tests/test_packaging_metadata.py, 52 passed, run the way the job
runs it (`--noconftest`).

2026-09-22 — IMPLEMENTATION: MODEL ZOO PICKER CATALOGUE TEARDOWN
The reported lingering ModelZoo JobRunner exposed a production omission:
close/reject stopped downloads but never retired catalogue results or stopped
the backend-probe timer; accept/done omitted catalogue cleanup too. All four
visible-dialog paths reproduced the pending-runner failure.
The picker now retires catalogue work in done(), through JobRunner.shutdown(0),
and stops probe polling. An in-flight read may finish under the existing safe
thread-retention mechanism; its result is discarded and cannot refresh the
closed dialog. Existing download teardown is unchanged.
Seven deterministic lifecycle cases cover visible exits and accept/reject/done
on never-shown dialogs, with a blocked fake HTTP read, a500ms close ceiling,
late-result checks and explicit worker release/retirement. Existing picker/
source-group tests now reject their never-shown dialogs before deletion and
leave live HTTP warming to the controlled lifecycle tests. This removes their
network-dependent parked-thread warnings without suppressing the leak checker.
All42 focused tests pass in5.69s without a leftover-worker report; seven final
lifecycle checks and repository fatal Ruff checks also pass. Full CI stays open.

2026-09-22 — IMPLEMENTATION: MOVIE EXPORT VALIDATION AND OWNERSHIP
The frozen coverage run expected _npz_to_movie to pass a4-D array to the video
writer. Real OpenCV instead failed in putText; passing a volume unconverted is
not a usable video contract. Export now refuses unsupported dimensions before
opening a writer, with the caller responsible for a slice or projection.
All frames must have matching nonempty spatial dimensions, supported pixel
types, finite float values, one filename each and a finite positive frame rate.
uint8/uint16 and finite floats retain their documented scaling; float64 now
follows float32. Filename stamps use owned RGB storage and cannot edit source
pixels, including read-only arrays. Failed writer opening is reported, and the
writer is always released if annotation or encoding raises.
Sixteen added checks failed before the fix; all68 focused movie/GIF/timelapse
checks now pass in12.67s. Real MP4/AVI container readback remains exercised;
the two-channel check now decodes actual red/green pixels rather than inspecting
source-code indentation. The4-D expectation is an explicit-refusal test. No
tracking algorithm, CI threshold or coverage baseline changed. Full CI is open.

2026-09-27 — NIGHTLY 3307cb8a8: SIX FAILURE FAMILIES AND THE gw0 NATIVE CRASH
Reproduced against run 36283739124's logs (the last to reach its test jobs).
- Dense channel map (r8/r9/stale_c pins): item 493 moved the Cellpose-SAM
  loop into _fill_cellpose_channel_positions and left a copy in
  generate_cellpose_masks. That generator now calls the helper too; the
  three pins follow the one-helper structure.
- log_call verbose-off test: is_verbose() reads _verbose, and the verbose
  preference defaults on, so any earlier test that applied preferences left
  it True. The test now sets it off itself.
- Organism pages [plasmodium]: a 30 ms wait. Under load the tiles are still
  narrowing at 30 ms ("Drug response imaging" 152 px wide, needs 158, 172
  once laid out; 21 of 40 builds on a loaded host). The test waits for the
  settled layout. Fonts are not the cause: DejaVu Sans, CI's substitute for
  Open Sans, needs 163 px of the 171 px it is given.
- Instruction index: tools/build_instruction_index.py regenerates it
  identical apart from the date on this tree; it was stale only in 43845277b.
- a_channel_brings_its_segmentation_settings: passes on this tree, alone and
  in the replayed gw0 order; its failure was on the previous agent's tree.
- Preloading timing test: flaky, not order-dependent. It failed as the FIRST
  file of a serial run (bisect 38-59) and passed 3/3 alone on the same host
  minutes later. It is skipped under xdist already; it now runs only when
  `-m timing` is asked for, the one request that says the machine is idle.
- NATIVE CRASH, FOUND AND FIXED: MeasurementScanPanel's reader threads
  emitted "read landed" on a _ReadRelay that was a CHILD of the panel. When
  the GUI thread destroyed the panel mid-read, the worker's emit ran on a
  QObject being freed. A replay of Qt shard 0 gw1's first 29 files under a
  CI-like fontconfig segfaulted there (_run_read, line 246). A 5000-cycle
  stress (build panel, start a read, delete the panel) crashed 4 of 4 runs
  with the child relay (segfault or abort) and 0 of 3 with the fix: one
  process-wide relay that is never destroyed, carrying a weak reference to
  its panel and skipping a panel that is gone. The same replay then ran to
  the end. The QImage::save -> QFactoryLoader -> moveToThread crash on gw0
  is most likely a later symptom of the same use-after-free (gw0 runs
  test_the_measurement_scan_panel.py first). It was NOT reproduced locally in
  ~6 replays, so this is not proven. A main-thread probe (theMainThread
  pointer, id and vtable checked before and after every test) saw no change
  across the whole gw0 list. spacr's moveToThread calls are worker setup
  plus three "come home" moves (sound, verbose_logger, logging_util). The
  every-screen sweep starts only Python threads (Annotate's path probes,

2026-10-10 10:07 EDT — Make Masks parent-source SIGSEGV: cause found, fix 18a308d33
Run 37943684517 Coverage0 (test_make_masks_parent_source_race_guards
[parent]) and Coverage7 (test_make_masks_center_pixel_puncta [False]) both
crash in the primary-mask waitUntil. The uploaded native cores give the same
main-thread stack: QEventLoop::exec -> sendPostedEvents -> QThreadWrapper::event
-> QObject::event (DeferredDelete) -> ~QThreadWrapper -> Shiboken::Object::
destroy -> releaseWrapper -> unregisterWrapper+0x50, reading the freed
wrapper's cptr array (QtCore.abi3/libshiboken offsets matched against the
identical 6.12.0 wheels; the collector's own gdb python diagnostic failed on
"No module named math"). The deleted QThread is PrimaryMaskSelector's
_SourceWorker, deleteLater'd in _finished with its Qt parent holding the last
Python reference. Shiboken 6.12 destroy() (unchanged on upstream dev) removes
the parent, which frees the wrapper, then calls releaseWrapper(self) whenever
hasWrapper(cppData) finds ANY entry at that address; a stale entry at a reused
address turns that into a use-after-free. That is why only loaded hosted
shards with a long test history crash and every local replay passes.
Fix (product): _retire() joins the worker and keeps a module reference to its
wrapper until the C++ half is gone, on the finished and drained-shutdown
paths; dead wrappers are released on the next retire. Regression
tests/qt/test_primary_mask_worker_outlives_its_cpp_half.py checks at
`destroyed` whether the wrapper still exists: [False] on the old code on both
PySide 6.10 and 6.12 (the crash window, deterministic), [True] with the fix.
Local reproduction of the SIGSEGV itself stayed negative: three parallel
PySide 6.12 + coverage xdist replays of the crashed worker's 13-file order
(496 passed each) and focused loops. Related selector/Make Masks/guard tests
pass. Other parented QThread subclasses deleted via deleteLater
(_MaskLoadWorker, _FolderJobWorker) have the same window; not changed here
because N673 is editing make_masks.py. Item 43 stays OPEN until hosted green.
